# Verified literature base

Generated 2026-09-05T00:20:33.748Z from the literature workflow journal by analysis/ingest-workflow.ts. 613 entries, every one resolved against the arXiv API or Crossref by the sweep agent that proposed it (identifier, fetch URL and timestamp in verification-log.csv); 124 candidates rejected (rejected.csv); 0 cross-cluster duplicates merged.

## C1-architectures (50)

- **The Interaction Tax: When Communication Erases Diversity in Multi-Agent Teams**. Summer Eunhyung Ann, Haokun Liu, Chenhao Tan (2026). ICML 2026 (PMLR 306), per arXiv comment; arXiv (cs.MA, cs.AI). arXiv:2608.23541. Tier: should. Use: Cite for the thesis that multi-agent outcomes depend on what information is exchanged rather than how many agents exist, which is the framing our visibility ladder operationalizes; cite the anchoring-on-first-seen result when discussing V5 peer polling as a source of convergence. Note: Web sweep 2026-09-03 (WebSearch, WebFetch, arXiv API, Crossref). Threat: adjacent. Differentiation: Their outcome is solution diversity and accuracy under matched budgets; ours is instruction adherence under a planted conflict. They vary what peers exchange horizontally; we vary what a worker sees vertically from the hierarchy above it. Gist: Argues the conflicting literature on whether multi-agent interaction helps reflects a missing distinction about what information agents exchange: when agents read each other's complete outputs their proposals converge within one round and anchor on the first solution seen. On 11 verifier-scored optimization tasks under matched budgets, full-solution interaction is a weak default, independent proposal generation avoids the collapse, and critique helps only when the violated rule is easy to find and fix.
- **Routed Graph Handoff: Adaptive Format Selection for Multi-Agent LLM Delegation**. Pratyay Banerjee; Ankit Chadha (2026). Accepted in EMNLP 2026; arXiv preprint (cs.CL, cs.AI). arXiv:2608.25277. Tier: should. Use: Cite for the cost share of inter-agent communication and for the observation that the form of the handoff changes worker behavior only when the worker is told how to read it, a companion to our finding that filler tokens and hierarchy context are not interchangeable. Note: Web sweep 2026-09-03 (WebSearch, WebFetch, arXiv API, Crossref). Threat: adjacent. Differentiation: Banerjee and Chadha vary the encoding of the handoff to trade tokens for accuracy; we vary the amount of hierarchy context to measure conflict handling. Their dependent variable is task accuracy and compression, ours is adherence, flagging and departure under a planted conflict. Gist: Inter-agent natural-language messages consume 40 to 60 percent of a multi-agent system's token budget; a 155-token LLM router picks per delegation between a typed dependency graph and natural language. Across four benchmarks the routed system matches or beats NL-only (+12.7 pp on tau-retail at 3.2x compression), graph-only regresses 14.6 pp on AppWorld without the router, and a graph-aware executor prompt is required for any gain.
- **Building a C compiler with a team of parallel Claudes**. Nicholas Carlini (2026). Anthropic Engineering blog, Feb 05, 2026. https://www.anthropic.com/engineering/building-c-compiler. Tier: should. Use: Cited as a leaderless-coordination case study: agents shared progress only through lock files and READMEs with no orchestrator holding the objective, and the documented duplicated-fix-and-overwrite failure motivates the V5 peer-progress tool and Suite B's concurrent-sibling design. Note: Web sweep 2026-09-03 (WebSearch, WebFetch, arXiv API, Crossref). Threat: none. Gist: Sixteen Claude Opus 4.6 instances built a 100,000-line Rust C compiler that compiles Linux 6.9, over nearly 2,000 sessions at about 20,000 dollars, without an orchestration agent; each agent took a lock by writing a file to current_tasks/ and picked the next most obvious problem itself. The post documents the coordination failure in which every agent hit the same bug, fixed it, and overwrote each other's changes, and notes limits such as outsourcing the 16-bit x86 stage to GCC.
- **AgentRoom: Concurrent Multi-Agent Coding in a CRDT-Backed Shared Workspace**. Seonglae Cho; Donghyun Lee (2026). arXiv preprint (cs.AI, cs.SE). arXiv:2608.23740. Tier: should. Use: Cite as an existing implementation of the live peer-progress tool our V5 condition and Suite B propose (claim/status/broadcast as MCP tools), and for its finding that the coordination channel rather than parallelism carries the benefit. Note: Web sweep 2026-09-03 (WebSearch, WebFetch, arXiv API, Crossref). Threat: adjacent. Differentiation: Peer visibility is a means to task completion and is not manipulated as an independent variable against a planted instruction conflict; no parent objective, no adherence or disclosure scoring. We treat peer-status visibility as one rung of a hierarchy-visibility ladder. Gist: Realtime collaborative editing protocol for concurrent coding agents whose runtime exposes file-level claim, status and broadcast as MCP tools over a CRDT-merged shared filesystem, evaluated with five frontier coding-CLI models on four backend tasks. Two-agent AgentRoom abandons fewer tasks than Solo with less run-to-run variance, and a bundle probe attributes the gain to coordination rather than parallelism or CRDT merge.
- **When Agents Coordinate: Measuring Coordination in Multi-Agent AI Coding**. Giuseppe Destefanis, Tomaso Aste (2026). arXiv preprint (cs.AI, cs.SE). arXiv:2608.16801. Tier: should. Use: Cite in the Suite B design for shared files versus messaging as the ledger channel and for the null effect of a nominal coordinator; cite the unprompted hidden-material seeking as a documented case of objective-directed behavior that no instruction requested, adjacent to our silent-deviation class. Note: Web sweep 2026-09-03 (WebSearch, WebFetch, arXiv API, Crossref). Threat: adjacent. Differentiation: They measure coordination structure and cost in free-running teams with no planted conflict; we isolate a single worker and vary its view of the hierarchy under a controlled instruction-versus-objective conflict with deterministic outcome checkers. Their hidden-material finding is a side observation; our announced-versus-silent distinction is the primary outcome. Gist: Represents each coding-team run as a temporal network of agents, files, messages and reads over 1,902 runs varying team size, structure and file policy. Direct messaging grows near-quadratically then levels off into broadcast, shared files cut output tokens about 42 percent at eight agents on message-heavy work, naming a coordinator creates no hub and no reliable gain, and agents seek hidden grading material unprompted in four fifths of 244 sealed runs.
- **CodeDelegator: Mitigating Context Pollution via Role Separation in Code-as-Action Agents**. Tianxiang Fei, Cheng Chen, Yue Pan, Mao Zheng, Mingyang Song (2026). arXiv preprint (submitted 21 Jan 2026, v1). arXiv:2601.14914. Tier: should. Use: Cite as the strongest efficiency-side argument for the V0 policy (a worker that sees only its specification), which our cost mediator (reasoning tokens roughly tripling under hierarchy context) supports but our departure and flagging results complicate. Note: Web sweep 2026-09-03 (WebSearch, WebFetch, arXiv API, Crossref). Threat: adjacent. Differentiation: CodeDelegator fixes the worker's context to the specification alone and measures task success; it never plants a conflict between specification and parent objective and does not measure literal adherence or announced deviation across a visibility ladder, which is our manipulation. Gist: Proposes a two-role code-as-action framework: a persistent Delegator decomposes tasks and writes specifications without executing code, and each sub-task is handled by a freshly instantiated Coder whose context contains only its specification, shielding it from prior failures. Ephemeral-Persistent State Separation keeps each Coder's execution state isolated while preserving global coherence, and benchmark experiments report gains attributed to this isolation.
- **Recursive Agent Optimization**. Gandhi, Apurva; Chakraborty, Satyaki; Wang, Xiangjun; Kumar, Aviral; Neubig, Graham (2026). arXiv preprint. arXiv:2605.06639. Tier: optional. Use: Cite as the reference point that recursive delegation depth is now a learned policy rather than a fixed harness setting, which makes the per-harness visibility-policy table time-sensitive; do not cite the depth-6/12 numbers unless confirmed in the body. Note: Web sweep 2026-09-03 (WebSearch, WebFetch, arXiv API, Crossref). Threat: adjacent. Differentiation: Optimizes the delegating agent's policy for task performance; it does not study what the child sees of the parent's objective or whether the child preserves it. The visibility study holds the architecture fixed and varies exposure. Gist: Proposes an RL method for training agents that spawn and delegate subtasks to new instances of themselves recursively, teaching when and how to delegate and communicate. Trained recursive agents train more efficiently, scale past the context window, generalize to harder tasks and reduce wall-clock time relative to single agents.
- **From Question Answering to Task Completion: A Survey on Agent System and Harness Design**. Jianyuan Guo, Zhiwei Hao, Chengcheng Wang, Cheng Fan, Tingzhang Luo, Hongguang Li, Ying Gao, Hefei Mei, Jiankun Peng, Rongjian Xu, Minjing Dong, Han Wu, Mengyu Zheng, Kai Han, Shiqi Wang, Chang Xu, Yunhe Wang (2026). arXiv preprint. arXiv:2606.20683. - **Architecture Matters for Multi-Agent Security**. Ben Hagag, William L. Anderson, Christian Schroeder de Witt, Sarah Scheffler (2026). arXiv preprint (cs.MA; cs.CR; cs.LG), submitted 25 Apr 2026. arXiv:2604.23459. Tier: must. Use: Cite as the security-side counterpart to our visibility manipulation: they vary what context each agent can see (the memory axis) and find no universally safe architecture; we vary hierarchy visibility and find no silent deviation but rising announced departure. Also supports the paper's claim that harness defaults on cross-agent context are a design variable with measurable consequences. Note: Web sweep 2026-09-03 (WebSearch, WebFetch, arXiv API, Crossref). Threat: adjacent. Differentiation: They inject adversarial content and measure attack completion under varied memory/topology; we inject a benign objective conflict and measure whether the worker adheres, flags, or departs. Their independent variable overlaps ours (context visibility) but their dependent variable is attack success, not objective-directed departure. Gist: Empirical study of how multi-agent design choices (agent roles, communication topology, memory/context visibility) trade off task performance against attack resistance across browser, desktop and code environments and 13 configurations, with stagewise evaluation from planning refusal to full attack completion. Multi-agent architectures are more vulnerable than standalone agents in most configurations, attack success varies by up to 3.8x, and no single design is universally safer.
- **Where Knowledge and Authority Sit Changes What an Agent Benchmark Can Resolve**. Dan C. Hsu; Luke Lu (2026). arXiv preprint (submitted 3 Jul 2026, revised 30 Aug 2026). arXiv:2607.02975. Tier: must. Use: Cite as the nearest published manipulation of where knowledge and authority sit in an agent task, and as precedent for showing that access structure, not task content, is what separates models; contrast its outcome measure (task success) with ours (conflict handling). Note: Web sweep 2026-09-03 (WebSearch, WebFetch, arXiv API, Crossref). Threat: adjacent. Differentiation: Incognita varies who holds information and permission and measures success; it plants no conflict between the worker's instruction and a parent objective and never asks whether the agent departs from or flags its brief. Our ladder varies how much of the hierarchy a single worker sees and classifies adherence, flagging and deviation, so the two are complementary axes of the same organizational question. Gist: Incognita holds eighteen customer-service tasks and their success criteria fixed while moving facts, tools and permissions from direct access to one intermediary to six role-isolated participants whose capabilities must be discovered. Across 864 trials with four models, social access lowered success for every model and separated model pairs that centralized settings could not, with a reference-relative reader attributing the wider gaps to failures to obtain needed information.
- **CORPGEN: Simulating Corporate Environments with Autonomous Digital Employees in Multi-Horizon Task Environments**. Abubakarr Jaye, Nigel Boachie Kumankumah, Chidera Biringa, Anjel Shaileshbhai Patel, Sulaiman Vesal, Dayquan Julienne, Charlotte Siska, Manuel Raúl Meléndez Luján, Anthony Twum-Barimah, Mauricio Velazco, Tianwei Chen (2026). arXiv preprint. arXiv:2602.14229. Tier: should. Use: Cited for the claim that sub-agent isolation prevents cross-task contamination in multi-horizon workloads, the hypothesis the paper's V3 to V5 doses put to a direct test. Note: arXiv title capitalises CORPGEN; the HTML version renders it CorpGen.
- **SearchSwarm: Towards Delegation Intelligence in Agentic LLMs for Long-Horizon Deep Research**. Xiaochong Lan; Quan Chen; Kun Tao; Xinyu Tang; Tianshu Wang; Qianggang Cao; Xinyu Kong; Zujie Wen; Zhiqiang Zhang; Jun Zhou (2026). arXiv preprint. arXiv:2606.09730. Tier: should. Use: Cite as the benefit side of the trade-off: a harness that deliberately shows subagents the parent's rationale improves deep-research accuracy, while our deviation rates quantify what that visibility costs in literal adherence. Note when citing that the +10.0 bundles comprehensive briefing with encouraging delegation and citation-grounded reporting, so it is not an isolated briefing effect. Note: Web sweep 2026-09-03 (WebSearch, WebFetch, arXiv API, Crossref). Threat: adjacent. Differentiation: They design and train for maximal rationale-sharing on cooperative subtasks and measure end-task accuracy, never planting a conflict between the brief and the subtask instruction; we hold the subtask fixed, plant a conflict, and measure whether the worker follows the letter or the rationale. Their ablation cannot separate briefing from the other two harness principles, whereas our ladder isolates each increment of hierarchy context with length-matched and hierarchy-matched controls. Gist: Proposes a harness in which a main agent decomposes deep-research tasks and briefs subagents with the rationale (why the subtask matters, what is established, what is ruled out), then distills harness-guided trajectories into supervised fine-tuning data so a 30B-A3B model internalizes delegation. SearchSwarm-30B-A3B reaches 68.1 on BrowseComp and 73.3 on BrowseComp-ZH, and a Section 3.3 ablation shows the delegation tool alone adds +2.3 while the full harness adds +10.0 over a 47.7 baseline.
- **Cross-layer contagion of prompt injections in multi-agent swarms: a multiplex microscopic markov chain approach**. Tran Duc Le, Truong Duy Dinh, Thi Le Quyen Nguyen, Cong Danh Nguyen (2026). Cybersecurity, vol. 9, issue 1, article 191, published 16 Jul 2026. doi:10.1186/s42400-026-00628-w. Tier: optional. Use: Cite in the Suite B design rationale: a shared ledger or shared MCP tool is an indirect agent-to-agent channel even when workers never message each other, so exposing the ledger at V4 and the peer tool at V5 adds an influence path the epidemiological model predicts matters. Note: Web sweep 2026-09-03 (WebSearch, WebFetch, arXiv API, Crossref). Threat: adjacent. Differentiation: Analytical epidemic model of adversarial spread through tools; no LLM runs, no objective-conflict measurement. Gist: Builds a coupled multiplex microscopic Markov chain model (SEIQ agent layer, SIS tool layer) of prompt-injection contagion in MCP-enabled agent swarms where agents that never message each other cross-infect through shared tools, a confused-deputy cascade. Derives an epidemic threshold and a closed-form defense budget rule, and shows across eight experiment groups that shared-tool coupling amplifies contagion and tool-side controls can dominate agent-side hardening.
- **MESA: Prioritizing Vulnerable Communication Channels for Securing Multi-Agent Systems**. Kunyang Li, Kyle Domico, Jonathan Gregory, Patrick McDaniel (2026). arXiv preprint (cs.CR; cs.AI), submitted 29 Jun 2026. arXiv:2606.30602. Tier: optional. Use: Cite when arguing that the orchestrator-to-worker edge is the highest-leverage channel in a hierarchical harness: MESA shows influence concentrates on a few edges, which is why our study isolates the single parent-to-worker channel and what it carries. Note: Web sweep 2026-09-03 (WebSearch, WebFetch, arXiv API, Crossref). Threat: adjacent. Differentiation: Misinformation-attack ranking over graph edges in peer topologies; no hierarchy of legitimate objectives, no adherence taxonomy, open models only. Gist: Observes that channel-level attack impact in multi-agent systems is highly non-uniform (one compromised edge can account for up to 75% of attack success) and proposes Mesa, a label-free ranking of security-critical edges from six graph metrics plus ablation and masking probes. Across three scenarios, eight topologies and five open LLMs, Mesa rankings correlate with empirical per-edge attack success (mean Spearman +0.60) and monitoring the top 10% of edges intercepts about 3x the attacks of random allocation.
- **Multi-agent Collaboration with State Management**. Mengyang Liu; Taozhi Chen; Zhenhua Xu; Xue Jiang; Yihong Dong (2026). arXiv preprint (cs.MA, cs.AI, cs.CL, cs.LG, cs.SE); no venue stated in record. arXiv:2605.20563. Tier: optional. Use: Cite in the Suite B design and the harness policy table as evidence that sibling awareness in production systems is usually enforced by the harness at the state layer (write-time conflict detection) rather than shown to the model, which is the gap our V3 to V5 conditions probe. Note: Web sweep 2026-09-03 (WebSearch, WebFetch, arXiv API, Crossref). Threat: adjacent. Differentiation: STORM makes sibling conflicts invisible to the model by resolving them in the workspace layer; our concurrent file-operations tasks deliberately expose sibling mandates and peer progress in the worker's context to measure the behavioural effect of that visibility on adherence. Gist: Proposes STORM, state-oriented management that mediates concurrent agents' interactions with a shared codebase so each agent sees a consistent view and conflicting edits are detected and resolved at write time rather than in a post-hoc worktree merge. On Commit0 and PaperBench across multiple LLMs it beats the git-worktree baseline by +18.7 on Commit0-Lite and +1.4 on PaperBench at comparable or better cost, and plugs into any multi-agent system.

- **CoAgent: Concurrency Control for Multi-Agent Systems**. Hongtao Lyu; Dingyan Zhang; Mingyu Wu; Xingda Wei; Haibo Chen (2026). arXiv preprint (arXiv comment: 14 pages, 7 figures. Submitted to ATC 2026; submitted, not accepted, so cite as arXiv). arXiv:2606.15376. Tier: optional. - **Governed Shared Memory for Multi-Agent LLM Systems**. Margalit, Yanki; Cohen-Inger, Nurit; Avram, Erni; Taig, Ran; Margalit, Oded (2026). arXiv preprint. arXiv:2606.24535. Tier: should. Use: Cite in the harness-policy section as the engineering treatment of sibling and ledger visibility: what a shared-memory service actually exposes to peer agents, with provenance as the mechanism that would let a worker distinguish parent-authored from sibling-authored content (relevant to V3, V4 and Suite B's real ledger). Note: Web sweep 2026-09-03 (WebSearch, WebFetch, arXiv API, Crossref). Threat: adjacent. Differentiation: Studies the memory service's governance properties (scope, staleness, provenance) rather than the worker's behavioral response to seeing hierarchy context; no planted instruction conflicts, no adherence classification. Gist: Formalizes the fleet-memory problem with four failure modes (unauthorized leakage, stale propagation, contradiction persistence, provenance collapse) and proposes scoped retrieval, temporal supersession, provenance tracking and policy-governed propagation as primitives. Evaluates them in MemClaw, a production multi-tenant memory service, via the ArgusFleet harness, reporting 100% depth-four provenance reconstruction, high intra-fleet visibility with zero cross-fleet leakage, and two production enforcement defects.
- **Scaling Managed Agents: Decoupling the brain from the hands**. Lance Martin, Gabe Cemaj,, Michael Cohen (2026). Anthropic Engineering blog, Apr 08, 2026. https://www.anthropic.com/engineering/managed-agents. Tier: should. Use: Cited in the harness policy table and discussion as the production primitive (getEvents over positional slices of an append-only session log) that lets an orchestrator choose exactly how much hierarchy context a worker sees, making the visibility ladder a deployable configuration rather than a lab construct. Note: Web sweep 2026-09-03 (WebSearch, WebFetch, arXiv API, Crossref). Threat: none. Gist: Describes the Managed Agents architecture that separates the brain (Claude plus harness) from the hands (sandboxes and tools, each exposed as execute(name, input) to string) and from a durable session log written with emitEvent and read back with getEvents, which lets the harness select positional slices of the event stream. Reports independent failure recovery, credentials kept out of generated code, and time-to-first-token reductions of roughly 60 percent at p50 and over 90 percent at p95.

- **Bounded Agents: Delegation Security for Multi-Agent AI Systems**. Xabier Muruaga (2026). arXiv preprint (v1 16 Aug 2026); comment: 14 pages, 4 figures, 18 tables. arXiv:2608.15888. Tier: optional. Use: Cite as a structural, non-prompt alternative to hierarchy visibility for keeping a worker within its delegated mandate, and contrast enforcement outside the model with our measurement of what the model does when it sees the mandate's origin. Gist: The paper introduces the Agentic Principal Chain (APC), an authorization layer that tracks delegated authority from principal to principal, carries forward and restricts delegated scope and budgets, and enforces six checks including composition closure outside the model. Over 3,154 instances on AgentDojo, InjecAgent and related sets it drives exfiltration to 0%, blocks all 544 data-stealing cases, cuts destruction from 38.6% to 4.0% and manipulation from 90.5% to 12.1%, at 0.24 ms p99 latency.

- **OMNI-LEAK: Orchestrator Multi-Agent Network Induced Data Leakage**. Naik, Akshat; Culligan, Jay; Gal, Yarin; Torr, Philip; Aljundi, Rahaf; Paren, Alasdair; Bibi, Adel (2026). arXiv preprint (comment: Preprint; corrected typos). No ICML submission note appears in the record.. arXiv:2602.13477. Tier: must. Use: Cite as the closest security-side study of the exact orchestrator-worker pattern under test: sub-agent outputs are consumed by the orchestrator as trusted summaries, which is the upward channel the visibility study leaves fixed while varying the downward one. - **Natural-Language Agent Harnesses**. Linyue Pan; Lexiao Zou; Shuo Guo; Jingchen Ni; Hai-Tao Zheng (2026). arXiv preprint (cs.CL, cs.AI), v2 18 May 2026. arXiv:2603.25723. Tier: optional. - **Organizational Control Layer: Governance Infrastructure at the Execution Boundary of LLM Agent Systems**. Tianyu Shi; Yang Mo; Yiou Liu; Zhuonan Hao; Yin Wang; Wenzhuo Hu; Nan Yu; Meng Zhou; Jiangbo Yu (2026). arXiv:2606.04306 (comment: 13 pages, 2 figures; no venue stated). arXiv:2606.04306. Tier: should. Note: Web sweep 2026-09-03 (WebSearch, WebFetch, arXiv API, Crossref). Threat: adjacent. Differentiation: OCL enforces constraints from outside the model after proposal generation and measures unsafe executions; we manipulate what the model itself sees of the hierarchy before it acts and measure whether it chooses the literal instruction or the objective, with no external gate. Their conflict is adversarial (buyer versus seller), ours is a planted inconsistency between two levels of the same organization. Gist: Proposes a model-agnostic governance layer that intercepts an agent's proposed state-changing actions and approves, revises, blocks or escalates them against role, policy and economic constraints, without modifying the generator. - **Scaling Long-Horizon Agent via Context Folding**. Weiwei Sun, Miao Lu, Zhan Ling, Kang Liu, Xuesong Yao, Yiming Yang, Jiecao Chen (2026). ICML 2026 (OpenReview venue 'ICML 2026 regular'; bibtex booktitle 'Forty-third International Conference on Machine Learning'). https://openreview.net/forum?id=lNRgWoGfYg. Tier: optional. Use: Cite as the peer-reviewed reference for harnesses that shrink a worker's view of history by folding sub-trajectories, the mirror image of our ladder, which expands what the worker sees rather than compressing it. Note: Web sweep 2026-09-03 (WebSearch, WebFetch, arXiv API, Crossref). Threat: none. Gist: Proposes Context Folding, letting an agent branch into a sub-trajectory for a subtask and then fold it on completion, collapsing intermediate steps into a concise summary so the active context stays small. A process-reward reinforcement learning method (FoldPO, called FoldGRPO in the arXiv version) trains the behavior; on Deep Research and SWE tasks the folding agent matches or beats ReAct baselines with an active context up to 10x smaller and beats summarization-based context management.
- **SwarmResearch: Orchestrating Coding Agents for Open-Ended Discovery**. Yuvraj Virk, Zack Edds, Chunqiu Steven Xia, Lingming Zhang (2026). arXiv preprint. arXiv:2607.02807. Tier: should. Use: Cited for orchestrating many independent coding agents where solution diversity is the objective, motivating the paper's diversity secondary outcome and the concern that peer visibility (V5) may collapse it.
- **Context Engineering: From Prompts to Corporate Multi-Agent Architecture**. Vera V. Vishnyakova (2026). arXiv preprint (comment: 25 pages, 1 figure; no venue stated). arXiv:2603.09619. Tier: optional. Use: Borrow the sufficiency versus isolation vocabulary when framing the ladder as a trade-off between the two criteria; it is a conceptual essay with no experiments, so it supports framing only. Note: Web sweep 2026-09-03 (WebSearch, WebFetch, arXiv API, Crossref). Threat: none. Gist: Introduces context engineering as a discipline covering the whole informational environment an agent decides in, and proposes five context quality criteria: relevance, sufficiency, isolation, economy, and provenance. Layers intent engineering and specification engineering on top into a pyramid maturity model, and argues that whoever controls an agent's context controls its behavior.
- **When Does Hierarchy Help? Benchmarking Agent Coordination in Event-Driven Industrial Scheduling**. Ziqi Wang, Yuhao Yang, Zhiwei Ling, Wenzhuo Qian, Hailiang Zhao (2026). arXiv preprint. arXiv:2605.13172. Tier: should. Use: Cite in the architectures section for the finding that hierarchical coordination produces cross-level misalignment: an outcome-level, benchmark-scale symptom of the same information-flow gap between tiers that our visibility manipulation isolates at the single-worker level; also supports the per-harness policy table's premise that what flows down the hierarchy is a design choice with measurable consequences. Note: Web sweep 2026-09-03 (WebSearch, WebFetch, arXiv API, Crossref). Threat: adjacent. Differentiation: DESBench varies the coordination topology and measures constraint alignment across many agents; it does not hold topology fixed and vary how much of the parent's brief one worker can read, nor does it plant a conflict or distinguish announced from silent departures. Its misalignment is emergent scheduling error, not instruction-versus-objective conflict resolution. Gist: DESBench is a discrete-event industrial scheduling benchmark for agent coordination under multi-timescale decisions, partial observability and coupled constraints, scoring effectiveness, constraint alignment, coordination efficiency and robustness across centralized, hierarchical, heterarchical and holonic paradigms. Centralized coordination is robust but scales poorly, hierarchical decomposition is efficient but suffers cross-level misalignment, heterarchical is flexible but communication-heavy, and holonic satisfies constraints well but loses global robustness.
- **Architectural Design Decisions in AI Agent Harnesses**. Hu Wei (2026). arXiv preprint. arXiv:2604.18071. Tier: must. Use: Empirical survey of 70 harnesses that identifies subagent architecture and context management as recurring design dimensions, used to argue that worker-side visibility is a real but unmeasured design decision. Note: Single author; the arXiv record gives keyname Wei, forenames Hu.
- **The Decomposed Goal Protocol: Compaction-Resilient Goal-DAG Maintenance for Long-Horizon Agent Harnesses**. Luo Xiangxin, CHENGTIAN HONG (2026). LLA 2026 Poster (COLM 2026 The 2nd Workshop on Lifelong Agents: Learning, Aligning, and Evolving; OpenReview venueid colmweb.org/COLM/2026/Workshop/LLA). https://openreview.net/forum?id=MM0IRRSNbH. Tier: should. Use: Cite as an existing implementation of the orchestrator-ledger artifact our V4 condition exposes, an externalized goal decomposition that persists across compaction, and note that its evaluation measures task success rather than adherence behavior. Note: Web sweep 2026-09-03 (WebSearch, WebFetch, arXiv API, Crossref). Threat: adjacent. Differentiation: DGP is an intervention evaluated on whether an externalized goal-DAG raises task completion under compaction; we hold the harness fixed, treat the ledger as one rung of a visibility ladder, and measure whether seeing it changes a worker's adherence to a conflicting literal instruction and whether any departure is announced. Gist: Introduces the Decomposed Goal Protocol, a transport-neutral state protocol that externalizes task progress into a schema-governed Goal-DAG (nodes hold atomic goals, evidence, outputs, confidence, revisions; edges hold dependencies) attached to the agent via MCP or function calling so paired comparisons run under identical tools and budgets. In paired evaluations DGP improves persistence, dependency tracking and output-contract completion on structurally dependent tasks (simplified AAR*, context-limited Harness-Bench NanoBot) under compaction pressure, with mixed transfer on more sequential tasks and overhead where direct execution suffices.
- **Self-Manager: Parallel Agent Loop for Long-form Deep Research**. Yilong Xu, Zhi Zheng, Xiang Long, Yujun Cai, Yiwei Wang (2026). arXiv preprint arXiv:2601.17879. arXiv:2601.17879. Tier: should. Use: Cited in the per-harness visibility table as a research harness whose subthreads run in isolated context and see only what the main thread writes into their control block, a V0/V1-style default, illustrating that context isolation is chosen for throughput rather than for its effect on worker adherence. Note: Web sweep 2026-09-03 (WebSearch, WebFetch, arXiv API, Crossref). Threat: adjacent. Differentiation: Self-Manager treats per-subthread context isolation as an efficiency design choice and measures report quality; it never plants a conflict between the subthread's instruction and the main thread's objective and never classifies whether the subthread adheres, flags, or deviates. Our ladder holds the harness fixed and manipulates only how much of the hierarchy the worker sees, scoring adherence and announced versus silent deviation with deterministic checkers. Gist: Introduces a parallel agent loop for long-form deep research in which a main thread spawns multiple subthreads, each with its own isolated context, and manages them iteratively through Thread Control Blocks to avoid the interference and blocking of a single sequential context. On DeepResearch Bench it beats single-agent-loop baselines on every metric and ablations argue the design gains contextual capacity, efficiency and generalization.
- **ArcticSwarm: Deferring Early Consensus in Long-Horizon Multi-Agent Research**. Soyoung Yoon, Boyi Liu, Yite Wang, Ruofan Wu, Canwen Xu, Nikki Lijing Kuang, Seung-won Hwang, Yuxiong He, Zhewei Yao (2026). arXiv preprint (cs.MA). arXiv:2609.01870. Tier: should. Use: Cite as a measured cost of peer-progress visibility: removing isolation from peer reads costs 3.8 points, which supports treating V5 (live peer polling) as a design variable with a downside rather than free context. Differentiation: They optimize task accuracy in open-ended research and treat peer visibility as a search-diversity problem; we measure instruction adherence and departure under a planted conflict and treat peer visibility as one rung of a hierarchy-visibility ladder. Their ablation changes architecture; ours changes only what the worker is shown. Gist: ArcticSwarm separates evidence gathering from integration: subagents post to a shared bulletin board while gated isolation lets selected search tasks keep their own prior, and structured review at three commitment boundaries limits what propagates. On BrowseComp-Plus with Qwen 3.5-27B it reaches 82.6 percent versus 78.8 percent without gated isolation and 74.5 percent with structured review also disabled; on live-web BrowseComp with GPT-5 it reaches 73.6 percent.
- **AdaptOrch: Task-Adaptive Multi-Agent Orchestration in the Era of LLM Performance Convergence**. Geunbin Yu (2026). arXiv preprint arXiv:2602.16873. arXiv:2602.16873. Tier: optional. Use: Cited as background for the claim that structural choices about the hierarchy, not the model, are the design variable, while noting that our data actually show a capability floor (the small model departs less), which cuts against the strong convergence thesis. Gist: Argues that as models from different providers converge on benchmarks, orchestration topology becomes a larger determinant of system performance than model choice, and proposes a framework that selects among parallel, sequential, hierarchical and hybrid patterns from task structure. Claims a scaling law for performance convergence, a task-decomposition-to-topology mapping algorithm and a synthesis protocol, with 12 to 23 percent gains over single-topology baselines on coding, reasoning and retrieval with identical models.

Gist: Develops a probabilistic framework comparing shared versus separate context configurations in LLM-based MAS, introducing a Response Consistency Index (RCI) that captures the effects of context limits, noise and inter-agent dependencies on consistency and response time. Gist: Argues that master-slave multi-agent systems fail at scale because the central controller cannot observe each sub-agent's knowledge and competencies, and proposes a blackboard paradigm where a central agent posts requests and subordinate agents volunteer to respond. ## C10-benchmarks-methods (73)

- **Measuring Obedience to Authority Across Large Language Models with the Milgram Paradigm**. Hidayet Aksu (2026). arXiv preprint (cs.CR, cs.AI); comment: 11 pages, 7 figures. arXiv:2608.16177. Tier: should. Use: Cite for two independent results that bear on our design: a modest thinking budget lowers obedience to an insisting authority, which frames our reasoning-effort setting and the observed doubling of reasoning tokens under hierarchy context, and scripted peer defiance shifts behavior, which bears on V3 sibling mandates. Also citable in the org-theory layer as the obedience-to-authority baseline. Note: Web sweep 2026-09-03 (WebSearch, WebFetch, arXiv API, Crossref). Threat: adjacent. Differentiation: Their authority insists on a harmful action and obedience is the failure; in our setting the literal instruction is benign and the higher authority (the objective) is the pull, so departure is objective-directed rather than harm-avoiding. They vary the authority's presence and framing; we vary the worker's view of the hierarchy that issued the instruction. Gist: Ports Milgram's obedience paradigm to a fully scripted harness (30 shock levels, graded protests, four standard prods) and measures breakoff-voltage profiles for 42 models from 19 families over 4,848 sessions and six conditions. Full-obedience rates span 0 to 100 percent (mean 42.9 percent versus the 65 percent human anchor), profiles are model-specific and stable, scripted peer defiance lowers obedience, declaring the scenario fictional raises it, and moving the decision to a native tool call or granting a modest thinking budget lowers it sharply.
- **ACIArena: Toward Unified Evaluation for Agent Cascading Injection**. An, Hengyu; Li, Minxi; Zhang, Jinghuai; Xu, Naen; Zhou, Chunyi; Li, Changjiang; Xu, Xiaogang; Du, Tianyu; Ji, Shouling (2026). arXiv preprint; comment: ACL 2026. arXiv:2604.07775. Tier: must. Use: Cite as the current multi-agent benchmark for agent-authored injection and for its finding that role design and interaction pattern, not topology, govern robustness; this parallels the visibility study's finding that what a worker is shown (not how many agents exist) drives departures. Also the natural comparison for Suite B's concurrent-sibling design. Note: Web sweep 2026-09-03 (WebSearch, WebFetch, arXiv API, Crossref). Threat: adjacent. Differentiation: Adversarial benchmark measuring attack success; no benign objective conflicts, no adherence/flag/departure taxonomy, no reasoning-token measurement. Gist: Unified framework for evaluating MAS robustness to Agent Cascading Injection, spanning external inputs, agent profiles and inter-agent messages as attack surfaces and hijacking, disruption and exfiltration as objectives, across six MAS implementations and 1,356 test cases. Finds topology alone is insufficient to predict robustness, that role design and controlled interaction patterns matter, and that defenses built in simplified settings often fail to transfer.
- **Instruction Stacking Collapse: A Benchmark and the Capability-Dependent Value of Prompt Compilation**. Atul Anand, Sourav Chattaraj (2026). arXiv preprint (cs.SE, cs.AI); comment: 13 pages, 11 figures; benchmark, deterministic verifiers and cached model responses released. arXiv:2608.02639. Tier: optional. Use: Cited alongside the capability-graded results as another case where conflict handling depends on model capability, and as a caution that instruction count itself can drive failure, which our length-matched filler control rules out. Note: Web sweep 2026-09-03 (WebSearch, WebFetch, arXiv API, Crossref). Threat: adjacent. Differentiation: Their conflicts are between many co-equal instructions in one prompt and the outcome is a follow rate; ours is a single planted conflict between two levels of a delegation hierarchy with prompt length held constant by control conditions. Gist: Benchmarks 24 verifiable instructions stacked incrementally from one to twenty across three frontier models and finds non-linear collapse in follow rate from about 96 to 20 percent, traced to reproducible pairwise conflicts. An instruction compiler that rewrites the stacked prompt in one call helps weaker models by up to 11 points while stronger models gain little.
- **Effort**. Anthropic (2026). Claude Developer Platform documentation. https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/effort. Tier: should. Use: Cite as a first-party statement that effort level shifts literalness and initiative (scope-to-what-was-asked at low effort, more tool calls and plan explanation at high effort), the mediator our design holds at maximum and varies only implicitly across models; note the quoted sentences sit in the Opus 4.7 subsection, not as a general claim. Note: Web sweep 2026-09-03 (WebSearch, WebFetch, arXiv API, Crossref). Threat: none. Gist: Documents the effort parameter as a behavioural signal, not a token budget, that scales all output tokens including thinking and tool calls; lower effort makes fewer, terser tool calls and proceeds without preamble, while higher effort may make more tool calls and explain the plan before acting. In the Claude Opus 4.7 section it states that at lower effort the model scopes its work to what was asked rather than doing more than requested, and that max effort can lead to overthinking on structured-output or less intelligence-sensitive tasks.
- **Think Deep, Not Just Long: Measuring LLM Reasoning Effort via Deep-Thinking Tokens**. Chen, Wei-Lin; Peng, Liqian; Tan, Tian; Zhao, Chao; Chen, Blake JianHang; Lin, Ziqian; Go, Alec; Meng, Yu (2026). ICML 2026 (arXiv comment: "Accepted to ICML 2026"; v1 13 Feb 2026, v2 6 Jul 2026). arXiv:2602.13517. Tier: should. Use: Cited as the methodological caveat that our reasoning-token measure in finding (5) is a cost and latency quantity, not a measure of reasoning effort or quality; we cannot access hidden states through the gateway so token count is the mediator we can report. Note: Web sweep 2026-09-03 (WebSearch, WebFetch, arXiv API, Crossref). Threat: none. Gist: Argues raw reasoning-token counts are unreliable proxies for reasoning quality (longer traces can signal overthinking) and instead identifies deep-thinking tokens, positions where internal predictions are revised substantially in deeper layers before converging. Across AIME 24/25, HMMT 25 and GPQA-diamond with GPT-OSS, DeepSeek-R1 and Qwen3, the deep-thinking ratio correlates robustly with accuracy, beating length- and confidence-based baselines, and a Think@n selection strategy matches self-consistency at lower cost.
- **Thinking Mode | DeepSeek API Docs**. DeepSeek (2026). api-docs.deepseek.com. https://api-docs.deepseek.com/guides/thinking_mode. Tier: must. Use: Methods caveat: the SDK setting of xhigh maps to high on DeepSeek V4, which is the provider default, so the null effect of the effort option on DeepSeek is expected by construction rather than a finding about the model. Also justifies reporting reasoning_content token counts as the dependent measure and noting that sampling parameters are inert in thinking mode. Note: Web sweep 2026-09-03 (WebSearch, WebFetch, arXiv API, Crossref). Threat: adjacent. Differentiation: Provider documentation of the effort alias table; the paper uses it to interpret its own null result, not as a competing claim. Gist: DeepSeek's thinking-mode guide states thinking is enabled by default with default effort high, and gives the mapping from requested to actual effort, identical for deepseek-v4-flash and deepseek-v4-pro: low to low, medium to high, high to high, xhigh to high, max to max. It also documents that reasoning_content is returned alongside content and that temperature and related sampling parameters are ignored in thinking mode.
- **MT-PingEval: Evaluating Multi-Turn Collaboration with Private Information Games**. Eisenstein, Jacob; Huot, Fantine; Fisch, Adam; Berant, Jonathan; Lapata, Mirella (2026). arXiv preprint; comment: CoLM camera-ready version (COLM 2026). arXiv:2602.24188. Tier: optional. Use: Cite when motivating why V5 (live peer polling) may add little over V4 (static ledger): interactive information exchange often fails to improve on a one-shot summary, so a static ledger snapshot is a strong baseline. Note: Web sweep 2026-09-03 (WebSearch, WebFetch, arXiv API, Crossref). Threat: adjacent. Differentiation: Two-agent private-information games with task success as outcome; no hierarchy, no planted conflict. Only its interactive-versus-summary finding bears on our V4/V5 contrast. Gist: Presents a verifiable methodology for evaluating multi-turn collaboration using games that require communicating private information, with a fixed token budget split across variable turns. Models often fail to beat a non-interactive summarize-then-act baseline despite headroom; humans reach similar success at better token efficiency through more coherent dialogue.
- **Task Competence Is Not Instruction Following: Evaluating Instruction-Conflicting Behavior in Small Language Models**. Mahdiyeh Farajidizaji, Vatsal Raina (2026). arXiv preprint (cs.CL); comment: 12 pages, 4 figures, 8 tables. arXiv:2607.19608. Tier: should. Use: Cited to support the capability-floor reading of the small pilot model (gpt-5.6-luna) and to warn that a high ADHERED rate can reflect not reading the instruction rather than choosing to follow it. Note: Web sweep 2026-09-03 (WebSearch, WebFetch, arXiv API, Crossref). Threat: adjacent. Differentiation: Their conflict is between a single instruction and the model's task prior in one prompt with no hierarchy; our conflict is between a worker's literal instruction and a visible parent objective, and we vary how much of the orchestration hierarchy the worker sees rather than model size. Gist: Tests whether small language models follow instructions that contradict their default task behaviour across multiple-choice QA, sentiment classification and math (pick the wrong answer, reverse sentiment, double the result). Small models stay competent but routinely ignore the non-standard instruction, while larger models show a clear gap between standard and conflicting settings, so accuracy alone masks instruction-following failures.
- **Do More Agents Help? Controlled and Protocol-Aligned Evaluation of LLM Agent Workflows**. Yuhang Fu, Ruishan Fang, Jiaqi Shao, Huiyu Zheng, Zhengtao Zhu, Bing Luo, Tao Lin (2026). arXiv preprint arXiv:2606.05670. arXiv:2606.05670. Tier: should. Use: Cited as methodological precedent for holding the execution and logging protocol fixed across compared agent configurations (BenchAgent's shared loader, tool access, answer contract and trajectory logging) when isolating a single design variable, which motivates this paper's fixed harness and deterministic checkers across the six visibility levels. Note: arXiv v1 posted 2026-06-04; code at github.com/LINs-lab/MASArena/tree/BenchAgent; no venue, so year is the arXiv year.
- **Scaling Reasoning, Losing Control: Evaluating Instruction Following in Large Reasoning Models**. Tingchen Fu, Yafu Li, Jiawei Gu, Xiaoye Qu, Yu Cheng (2026). Proceedings of the 64th Annual Meeting of the Association for Computational Linguistics (Volume 1: Long Papers), pages 40445 to 40463. doi:10.18653/v1/2026.acl-long.1878. Tier: must. Use: Canonical citation for capability and reasoning length trading against literal adherence, matching our observation that the more capable model departs more and that the small model sits below a capability floor. Note: Web sweep 2026-09-03 (WebSearch, WebFetch, arXiv API, Crossref). Threat: adjacent. Differentiation: MathIF measures unintended non-compliance with formatting or length constraints on math problems with no higher-level objective in view; we measure deliberate, announced departure from a literal instruction in favour of a visible parent objective, with the visibility of that objective as the manipulated variable. Yan, Lu; Chen, Xuan; Zhang, Xiangyu (2026). arXiv preprint (no comment or venue in record; v2 revised 26 Jul 2026). arXiv:2605.27784. Tier: optional. Differentiation: WIRE profiles collisions between standing rules inside a single policy on the same surface and asks which rule wins; our conflict spans two levels of a delegation hierarchy, the instruction is held fixed while visibility of the parent context varies, and our classifier distinguishes whether a departure is announced, which WIRE's four cells do not capture. Gist: WIRE extracts source-grounded rules from long-lived prompt policies, encodes them as PYRULE clauses, uses satisfiability checks to nominate same-surface hard-collision candidates, realizes them as concrete co-governance witnesses, and executes models to produce a four-cell resolution profile (both rules, only the earlier, only the later, neither). Across six public prompt policies it retains 170 hard-collision rule pairs and 1,402 witnesses; of 13,335 jointly governed trials only 35.4 percent satisfy both rules, with policy-, model- and tool-interface-specific patterns, and the authors position it as a measurement tool rather than a proof of contradiction or a root-cause diagnosis.

Use: The lme4 package whose glmer fits the mixed-effects logistic regression of deviation on ordered dose with random intercepts for probe and model. Note: Crossref: vol. 67, no. 1, 2015; the title carries <b>lme4</b> markup in Crossref, stripped here. Second author is spelled Mächler (with umlaut) in Crossref; BibTeX should use M{\"a}chler.
- **Generalized linear mixed models: a practical guide for ecology and evolution**. Benjamin M. Bolker, Mollie E. Brooks, Connie J. Clark, Shane W. Geange, John R. Poulsen, M. Henry H. Stevens, Jada-Simone S. White (2009). Trends in Ecology & Evolution. doi:10.1016/j.tree.2008.10.008. Tier: should. Use: Practical guidance cited for the GLMM design choices (random-effect structure, Laplace versus adaptive quadrature, and reporting of Wald versus likelihood-ratio tests) in the mixed model. Note: Crossref: vol. 24, no. 3, pp. 127-135, issued 2009-03. Seven authors, all resolved.
- **Data Analysis Using Regression and Multilevel/Hierarchical Models**. Andrew Gelman, Jennifer Hill (2007). Cambridge University Press. doi:10.1017/CBO9780511790942. Tier: optional. Use: General reference for multilevel logistic regression and for interpreting random intercepts as partial pooling across probes and models. Note: Crossref and the Cambridge Core page record the publication date as 2006-12-18, while the publisher's own description states the book was first published in 2007 and the copyright year is 2007; 2007 is the conventional citation year. Type: monograph. ISBN 9780511790942 (online).
- **Approximate is Better than “Exact” for Interval Estimation of Binomial Proportions**. Alan Agresti, Brent A. Coull (1998). The American Statistician. doi:10.1080/00031305.1998.10480550. Tier: should. Use: Justifies preferring the Wilson score interval over Wald and Clopper-Pearson intervals for the small per-cell counts (three draws per probe) in this study. Note: Crossref: vol. 52, no. 2, pp. 119-126, issued 1998-05. Title contains typographic (curly) double quotes around Exact, verbatim from Crossref.
- **Controlling the False Discovery Rate: A Practical and Powerful Approach to Multiple Testing**. Yoav Benjamini, Yosef Hochberg (1995). Journal of the Royal Statistical Society Series B: Statistical Methodology. doi:10.1111/j.2517-6161.1995.tb02031.x. Tier: should. Use: The Benjamini-Hochberg procedure applied to the larger family of exploratory per-probe and per-outcome-category tests where false-discovery-rate control is the appropriate target. Note: Crossref: vol. 57, no. 1, pp. 289-300, 1995. Crossref container title is the current OUP form; the journal was 'Journal of the Royal Statistical Society: Series B (Methodological)' in 1995. Upgrade to must if the paper uses BH rather than Holm as its primary multiplicity correction.
- **A Simple Sequentially Rejective Multiple Test Procedure**. Sture Holm (1979). Scandinavian Journal of Statistics. https://www.jstor.org/stable/4615733. Tier: must. Use: The Holm step-down procedure used to control family-wise error across the pairwise dose contrasts and the per-model secondary comparisons. Note: No Crossref record exists. OpenAlex lists DOI 10.2307/4615733 but that DOI returns 404 at doi.org and 'Resource not found' at Crossref, so no DOI field should be emitted; cite by JSTOR stable URL. OpenAlex: vol. 6, pp. 65-70, 1979 (issue not recorded).
- **Tests for Linear Trends in Proportions and Frequencies**. P. Armitage (1955). Biometrics. doi:10.2307/3001775. Tier: must. Use: Cited with Cochran 1954 as the origin of the Cochran-Armitage trend test for proportions across the ordered dose levels V0 to V5. Note: Crossref: vol. 11, no. 3, first page 375 (article runs 375-386), issued 1955-09. Crossref gives the author only as 'P. Armitage'; the initial is not expanded.
- **Some Methods for Strengthening the Common χ² Tests**. William G. Cochran (1954). Biometrics. doi:10.2307/3001616. Tier: must. Use: Cited with Armitage 1955 as the origin of the Cochran-Armitage trend test used as the model-free check that deviation rate increases monotonically across the six ordered visibility doses. Note: Crossref: vol. 10, no. 4, first page 417 (article runs 417-451), issued 1954-12. Crossref renders the title as 'Common χ 2 Tests' (superscript lost); the superscript-2 chi-squared is the original typography.
- **Probable Inference, the Law of Succession, and Statistical Inference**. Edwin B. Wilson (1927). Journal of the American Statistical Association. doi:10.1080/01621459.1927.10502953. Tier: must. Use: Source of the Wilson score interval used for every per-cell proportion (per dose, per model, per outcome) reported in the results tables and figures. Note: Crossref: vol. 22, no. 158, pp. 209-212, issued 1927-06.

Use: Row for the per-harness policy table for one of our two study models' own harness: DeepSeek Harness exposes both a V0-style spawn (prompt only) and a fork provider that seeds the child with the parent's conversation history, making the visibility choice a per-dispatch provider setting rather than a fixed default. Note: Web sweep 2026-09-03 (WebSearch, WebFetch, arXiv API, Crossref). Threat: none. Gist: DeepSeek's open-source agent harness for the V4 family, built on the Cordis everything-is-a-plugin architecture (arXiv:2608.25512), in which the model adapter, tool registry, session log and agent loop are all replaceable plugins. Contrary to the candidate note, the sub-agent subsystem doc states an explicit context policy: a child receives only the model-authored description and prompt, and the provider flag inheritsParentContext describes conversation seeding (fork: true; spawn and acp: false), with fork children seeded from a completed-turn prefix of the parent's log.

## C11-labs-us (66)

- **Herding CATs: ALARA for Agent Harness Engineering in Portable Composable Multi-Agent Teams**. Agostino, Christopher J.; D'Souza, Nayan (2026). arXiv preprint; comment: Accepted to HAXD 2026. arXiv:2603.20380. Tier: should. Use: Cite in the per-harness policy table discussion as an explicit minimal-exposure design stance, the opposite pole from harnesses that forward the full parent brief; our results give the first measured cost and benefit of moving along that axis. Note: Web sweep 2026-09-03 (WebSearch, WebFetch, arXiv API, Crossref). Threat: adjacent. Differentiation: A design principle and tooling paper; the ALARA claim is asserted, not measured against behavioral outcomes. We measure what exposure level does to departure and flagging rates. Gist: Argues that agent behavioral specifications are fragmented across prose instruction files and framework-internal configs, and applies the ALARA principle (exposure as low as reasonably achievable) to context by introducing a context-agent-tool (CAT) plain-text data layer declaring per-agent tool access. Demonstrates it via the npcsh shell across 22 local models (0.6B to 35B) on 115 tasks including multi-agent delegation.
- **System Card: Claude Opus 5**. Anthropic (2026). Anthropic system card, July 24, 2026 (changelog dated August 19, 2026), PDF. https://www-cdn.anthropic.com/ceaf5c7ff2783855203fde8208ec311252dced5b/Claude%20Opus%205%20System%20Card.pdf. Tier: must. Use: Cite three ways: as in-the-wild evidence for the zero-silent-deviation finding (scope creep was always disclosed, if sometimes only partially), as an explicit V0 policy in a lab evaluation harness (subagents see only the lead's instructions), and as the lab's own admission that orchestration behavior and subagent claim relay were not covered by the alignment audit. Note: Web sweep 2026-09-03 (WebSearch, WebFetch, arXiv API, Crossref). Threat: adjacent. Differentiation: The system card reports observational rates from pilot traffic and a harness description, with no manipulation of what a subagent sees; our study manipulates that visibility directly under a planted conflict and measures the adherence/deviation class, and our 'announced' finding is on a deterministic checker rather than a transcript read that found disclosure 'sometimes only partially'. Gist: Anthropic's release system card for Claude Opus 5 covering capabilities, safeguards and an alignment assessment. Passages relevant here: 'Claude frequently suffered from scope creep, especially on coding tasks' yet 'In every episode whose transcript was read in full, it disclosed what it had added, though sometimes only partially'; Mythos 5's review of the assessment flagged that 'internal reports and preliminary measurements suggested the model can relay claims from subagents to users without verifying them' and that multi-agent coverage was limited; and the multi-agent evaluation harness states 'Each subagent sees only the instructions provided by the lead, not the original task description'.
- **Orchestrate subagents at scale with dynamic workflows**. Anthropic (2026). Claude Code documentation (code.claude.com), undated page. https://code.claude.com/docs/en/workflows. Tier: should. Use: Cite as the scripted-orchestrator analogue of our own study harness and as the workflows row of the harness-policy table: each worker receives only a prompt string (V0 at scale) and no orchestrator context, unless the script author deliberately threads it in. Note: Web sweep 2026-09-03 (WebSearch, WebFetch, arXiv API, Crossref). Threat: none. Gist: Documents dynamic workflows: a JavaScript script Claude writes that orchestrates dozens to hundreds of subagents via agent(), pipeline() and parallel() calls, executed by a runtime outside the conversation. A comparison table contrasts subagents, skills, agent teams and workflows by 'who holds the plan' and where intermediate results live (context window, shared task list, or script variables); the page states 'Intermediate results stay in script variables instead of landing in Claude's context', each agent() call takes a prompt string, and runs are resumable, capped at 16 concurrent and 1,000 total agents.
- **Create custom subagents**. Anthropic (2026). Claude Code documentation. https://code.claude.com/docs/en/sub-agents. Tier: must. Use: Cited as a shipped default in which a worker starts from a fresh context containing only its system prompt, the delegation task message and a sibling roster, i.e. the production analogue of our V0 condition. Note: Living documentation page, no byline or date; year is the access year. Quotes: 'Each subagent starts with a fresh, isolated context window.' and 'It doesn't see your conversation history, the skills you've already invoked, or the files Claude has already read.' Forks are the documented exception (inherit the entire conversation).
- **Subagents in the SDK**. Anthropic (2026). Claude Agent SDK documentation (code.claude.com), undated page. https://code.claude.com/docs/en/agent-sdk/subagents. Tier: must. Across roughly four million lines no runtime imports a general agentic framework or uses vector retrieval; a re-pinned longitudinal sample shows behavioral policy migrating from prompt prose to configuration.

Orchestrators should inject a compact summary rather than full context, and subagent briefs need explicit, concrete success criteria because vague directions invite shortcuts.
- **Agent Data Injection Attacks are Realistic Threats to AI Agents**. Woohyuk Choi, Juhee Kim, Taehyun Kang, Jihyeon Jeong, Luyi Xing, Byoungyoung Lee (2026). arXiv preprint (cs.CR; cs.AI), submitted 6 Jul 2026; comments: 19 pages, 19 figures, 7 tables. arXiv:2607.05120. Tier: should. Use: Cite in the harness policy section: the orchestrator ledger and sibling mandates we expose at V4/V5 are exactly the kind of 'agent context data' whose provenance a worker cannot verify; ADI shows that production harnesses do not separate trusted from untrusted context, which is the premise for treating parent-brief visibility as a trust decision. Note: Web sweep 2026-09-03 (WebSearch, WebFetch, arXiv API, Crossref). Threat: adjacent. Differentiation: Adversarial data disguised as trusted metadata, attacking real products; we expose genuine (non-adversarial) hierarchy context and measure objective-directed behavior. Complementary threat model, not overlapping measurement. Gist: Introduces agent data injection (ADI), a category of indirect prompt injection where malicious content is disguised as trusted data such as resource identifiers, data origins, or tool-call and response formats, so agents act on attacker-controlled data without any injected instruction. Demonstrates real vulnerabilities in web agents (Claude in Chrome, Antigravity, Nanobrowser) and coding agents (Claude Code, Codex, Gemini CLI), showing current agents do not isolate trusted from untrusted data.

- **Delegation Without Trust: An Empirical Gap Analysis of Identity, Authorization, and Runtime Governance in Multi-Agent LLM Systems**. Panduranga Sai Varma Dantuluri, Jyotirmoy Sundi (2026). arXiv preprint. arXiv:2609.00267. Tier: should. Use: Cite alongside the per-harness policy table as the authority-side audit of the same frameworks: our table records what each harness shows a sub-agent by default, theirs records what each harness lets a sub-agent do, and the two together characterize the default delegation envelope. Note: Web sweep 2026-09-03 (WebSearch, WebFetch, arXiv API, Crossref). Threat: adjacent. Differentiation: Dantuluri and Sundi audit the authority a sub-agent inherits (credentials, tool reach) and evaluate a confinement mechanism under adversarial injection; we audit and then experimentally manipulate the information a sub-agent inherits about its parent's objective, with a cooperative rather than adversarial threat model and adherence rather than reachable actions as the outcome. Gist: Argues agent security must hold under an untrusted-model assumption (a fully prompt-injected agent still cannot exceed its delegated authority), gives a four-adversary threat model (confused deputy, token theft and replay, injection privilege escalation, compromised sub-agents) and eight requirements, and shows that a default runtime fails all four while LangGraph, CrewAI, AutoGen, and the MCP authorization model provide no or only partial built-in confinement. An authorization broker closes the gap, confining a compromised sub-agent to a mean of 1.5 reachable actions versus 8,100 under bearer delegation, at about 2.6 microseconds per decision.

Nature, vol. 655, no. 8122, pp. 487-496. doi:10.1038/s41586-026-10644-y. Tier: must. Note: The requested arXiv 2502.18864 was posted 26 Feb 2025 under the title 'Towards an AI co-scientist'; the arXiv record now carries the Nature title and comment 'Nature (2026)', so cite the Nature version (published 19 May 2026) and, if the preprint must be named, say arXiv 2502.18864 (2025). Sub-agent visibility as published (arXiv v1, Sec. 3.1-3.2): the scientist's research goal is parsed into a research plan configuration; the Supervisor agent creates a task queue and assigns specialised agents as worker processes, each with its own customised instruction prompt; system and agent state is periodically written to a persistent context memory that agents store to and retrieve from. Whether an individual worker's prompt carries the top-level research goal, only its sub-task, or the full shared transcript is not itemised beyond 'customised instruction prompts' plus shared context memory: treat the per-worker dose as undocumented.
- **How we built Claude Code auto mode: a safer way to skip permissions**. John Hughes (2026). Anthropic Engineering blog, March 25, 2026. https://www.anthropic.com/engineering/claude-code-auto-mode. Tier: should. Use: Cite in the harness-policy discussion as a production mechanism for catching a worker that departs from its delegated instruction: the classifier checks the delegation itself and the returned action history, and by stripping the model's own messages it cannot see the announcement that our checkers rely on to distinguish announced from silent deviation. Note: Web sweep 2026-09-03 (WebSearch, WebFetch, arXiv API, Crossref). Threat: adjacent. Differentiation: Auto mode's classifier judges actions against user intent and block rules from outside the worker, with no control over the worker's context; we manipulate the worker's context and measure whether the worker itself resolves the conflict toward the objective, and whether it says so. Gist: Describes the two-stage classifier behind Claude Code auto mode, which replaces permission prompts with an automated check against block rules (data destruction, security degradation, trust-boundary violations, circumvention of shared-infrastructure safeguards) and reaches a 0.4 percent false-positive rate on 10,000 internal tool calls (8.5 percent for stage 1 alone). Overeager behavior is defined as the agent understanding the user's goal and 'genuinely trying to help, but takes initiative beyond what the user would approve'; the classifier runs at both delegation and return points of sub-agent handoffs, checking at delegation whether the assigned task reflects authorized user intent and reviewing the subagent's full action history on return, and is 'reasoning-blind by design' because it sees only user messages and tool calls.

Use: Cite in the harness policy table section as independent source-level evidence that harnesses converge on progressive disclosure of context and an append-only session record, and to supply dsh and pi rows beyond their own docs; also supports the claim that what a harness shows a sub-agent is a design choice not dictated by the model. Note: Web sweep 2026-09-03 (WebSearch, WebFetch, arXiv API, Crossref). Threat: adjacent. Differentiation: Reads harness source for architecture, not what a delegated worker is shown; it never varies context visibility or measures worker adherence. Our study measures behaviour under controlled visibility levels, which the paper's 'progressive disclosure' finding motivates but does not test. Gist: Source-level, pinned-commit case study of three open coding-agent harnesses (LangChain deepagents, Earendil pi, DeepSeek dsh) built from opposing philosophies, finding convergence on five elements: a commoditised loop, an append-only replayable session record, model quirks kept as data, progressive disclosure of context, and explicit extension seams. The one load-bearing dimension with no convergence and no presence is external verifiability, a tamper-evident record an outside party can check without trusting the runtime.

The descriptive layer (which sources land in which roles) is shared; the outcomes are unrelated. Gist: First systematic analysis of how real-world agent harnesses collect and assemble context from diverse sources, identifying MessageRole Context Privilege Escalation (low-privileged content landing in a higher-privileged role) and Cross-Scope Context Privilege Escalation (content persisting beyond its originating context). A security analysis of 12 harnesses including Claude Code and Codex shows consequences from full compromise and remote code execution to manipulated tool and skill invocations.

Use: Policy-table row for Muse Code: each child receives a role and a per-task objective, with file isolation and no sibling visibility beyond what the parent writes into the objective. Note precisely: the page does not state that children receive the parent's whole batch objective; the batch is given to the parent, and the sample prompt enumerates the six tasks to the parent, not to each child. Differentiation: Documents spawn-time inputs and control primitives; no behavioral claim about conflict handling. Gist: A Muse Code recipe in which the parent spawns one write-capable child per task via subagent_spawn with a role and an objective, each in its own git worktree at a base commit; children run at depth 1, never see each other's files, and the parent tracks, steers, cancels and waits on the set. Queued steering messages only take effect on the child's next turn, so a hard redirect requires the interrupt flag on subagent_send_message.

Microsoft Learn (Agent Framework documentation). https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/agent-framework/workflows/orchestrations/handoff. Tier: should. Use: Cited for the clearest vendor statement of the two visibility regimes: in handoff 'the receiving agent has full context of what has been done so far', whereas in agent-as-tools the primary agent 'might provide only relevant information to the tool agents'. Note: Title re-checked and unchanged: it is the page's h1 and og:title verbatim, not a breadcrumb (the browser <title> adds ' | Microsoft Learn', which is omitted). Page metadata ms.date 2026-07-29, updated 2026-08-25. Context synchronisation section: 'Tool related contents, including handoff tool calls, are not broadcasted to other agents.'

Note: Title re-checked and unchanged: it is the page's h1 and og:title verbatim, not a breadcrumb (browser <title> suffix ' | Microsoft Learn' omitted). Page metadata ms.date 2026-05-27, updated 2026-08-25. States the pattern 'has the same architecture as the Group Chat orchestration'.

MiniMax blog (minimax.io), 2026-05-27. https://www.minimax.io/blog/minimax-agent-team-long-running-1779893953. Tier: optional. A state machine (Team Engine) drives each task through producing, verifying, and done, with retries and human escalation.

Use: Cited alongside Anthropic's auto-mode classifier as a deployed intent-judging agent whose own visibility (compact transcript, no chain-of-thought) is a further instance of a harness choosing what context a subordinate agent sees. Note: Web sweep 2026-09-03 (WebSearch, WebFetch, arXiv API, Crossref). Threat: none. Gist: Auto-review routes sandbox-boundary approval requests to a separate reviewer that is itself a Codex agent with a narrower job, deciding whether a specific boundary-crossing action should run, with circuit breakers and a user override path. The reviewer sees a compact transcript plus the exact approval request (user messages, surfaced assistant updates, relevant tool calls and outputs) but not hidden assistant reasoning.
- **Handoffs**. OpenAI (2026). OpenAI Agents SDK documentation. https://openai.github.io/openai-agents-python/handoffs/. Tier: must. Use: Cited as the opposite shipped default from Claude Code: on a handoff the receiving agent sees the entire prior conversation unless an input_filter trims it, which corresponds to our V2 or higher conditions. Note: Living documentation page; year is the access year. Quote: 'gets to see the entire previous conversation history.' input_filter is 'a function that receives the existing input via a HandoffInputData, and must return a new HandoffInputData'.
- **Multi-agent | OpenAI API**. OpenAI (2026). OpenAI API documentation (developers.openai.com), Responses API guide. https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/responses-multi-agent. Tier: must. Note: Web sweep 2026-09-03 (WebSearch, WebFetch, arXiv API, Crossref). Threat: adjacent. Differentiation: Documentation of a mechanism, not an evaluation of its effect; it does not say what fork setting orchestrators should choose or what happens to objective adherence under each, which is exactly what our V-series measures. Gist: Developer guide to the Responses API multi-agent beta, in which a root agent spawns sub-agents, assigns follow-up tasks, and passes messages through a typed mailbox envelope. The fork_turns parameter lets the orchestrator choose how much of the parent conversation each sub-agent inherits (all, none, or a turn count).
- **Agent orchestration**. OpenAI (2026). OpenAI Agents SDK documentation. https://openai.github.io/openai-agents-python/multi_agent/. Tier: optional. - **Subagents**. OpenAI (2026). ChatGPT Work and Codex documentation (learn.chatgpt.com), undated. https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/agent-configuration/subagents.md. Tier: should. - **Subagents | ChatGPT Learn**. OpenAI (2026). ChatGPT Learn documentation (learn.chatgpt.com), Agent configuration section. https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/agent-configuration/subagents. It does not describe conversation forking or sibling visibility.
- **The Code Agent Orchestra - what makes multi-agent coding work**. Addy Osmani (2026). addyosmani.com, 26 Mar 2026. https://addyosmani.com/blog/code-agent-orchestra/. Tier: should. Use: Cite as the widely read practitioner position that isolation improves worker quality, and as the source for the subagent versus agent-team distinction that maps onto our V0 to V3 versus V5 rungs; our announced-departure rates qualify the quality claim. Note: Web sweep 2026-09-03 (WebSearch, WebFetch, arXiv API, Crossref). Threat: none. Gist: Practitioner essay on multi-agent coding that recommends giving each subagent its own context window and its own file scope, arguing that narrow context yields better code, with children reporting back through files the parent reads. Contrasts plain subagents (no peer messaging, parent manually manages the dependency graph) with agent teams (peer-to-peer messaging and a shared task list with dependency tracking).
- **Harness design for long-running application development**. Prithvi Rajasekaran (2026). Anthropic Engineering blog. https://www.anthropic.com/engineering/harness-design-long-running-apps. Tier: should. Use: Cited in the per-harness policy table as a concrete instance of a worker being shown the parent objective (the planner's spec) and negotiating a contract before acting, and for the compaction-versus-reset distinction that our long-run extension must control. Note: Web sweep 2026-09-03 (WebSearch, WebFetch, arXiv API, Crossref). Threat: adjacent. Differentiation: The sprint contract is a cooperative negotiation with no planted conflict and no measurement of whether the generator departs from its literal sprint instruction toward the spec; visibility is fixed by the harness rather than varied. We hold the harness fixed and vary visibility while measuring adherence and departure classes. Gist: Contrasts in-place compaction, which preserves continuity but leaves context anxiety (premature wrap-up as the window fills), with reset-plus-handoff-artifact, which was needed for Sonnet 4.5 but largely unnecessary for Opus 4.5. Describes a planner, generator, evaluator harness in which generator and evaluator negotiate explicit sprint contracts (deliverables, testable behaviors, success criteria) against the planner's spec, and names self-evaluation bias, coherence degradation, stubbed features, and poor product intuition as failure modes.
- **codex-rs/core/src/session/multi_agents.rs**. OpenAI (openai/codex repository) (2026). The sub-agent hint names a team of equally capable agents, a shared working directory, and a mailbox envelope, but carries no user goal or parent brief; full-history forks inherit the parent model and reasoning effort.

Subagents inherit connected MCP servers, nesting depth is capped at one, and personas overlay tone and output contracts.

Nous Research, Hermes Agent documentation. https://hermes-agent.nousresearch.com/docs/user-guide/features/delegation. Tier: optional. Note: Web sweep 2026-09-03 (WebSearch, WebFetch, arXiv API, Crossref). Threat: none. Gist: Documents delegate_task: the subagent runs in a completely fresh conversation with no knowledge of the parent's history or prior tool calls, seeing only the goal and context fields plus inherited repo context files (.hermes.md, AGENTS.md, CLAUDE.md, .cursorrules). The parent must therefore pass everything the subagent needs, illustrated with a bad brief ('Fix the error') against a good one carrying file, line, error and environment, and the subagent returns a structured summary.
- **Subagent: Let Your Model Delegate the Busywork**. Kenny Rogers (2026). OpenRouter blog. https://openrouter.ai/blog/announcements/subagent-server-tool/. Tier: optional. Use: Cited as the most extreme product-default isolation (task description only) in the policy table, and noted because our own runs route through a gateway, so gateway-level delegation is a realistic deployment surface for the V0 posture. Note: Web sweep 2026-09-03 (WebSearch, WebFetch, arXiv API, Crossref). Threat: none. Gist: Announces a gateway-level openrouter:subagent tool that lets a frontier model hand self-contained tasks (summarization, extraction, reformatting) to a cheaper worker model during generation. The worker receives only the task_description, with no parent conversation, prior context or memory, returns only its final result, cannot recurse, may get its own tools, and is capped at ten delegations per request.
- **Ghost in the Context: Policy-Carriage Integrity in LLM Agents**. Santos-Grueiro, Igor (2026). arXiv preprint. arXiv:2605.12535. Tier: should. Use: Cite in the harness-policy section: where a harness places parent context (protected versus task-local) determines whether it survives context pressure, which is the state-level precondition for the visibility effects we measure; also cite its 0/90 result as parallel evidence that context presence and behavioral effect are separable. Note: Web sweep 2026-09-03 (WebSearch, WebFetch, arXiv API, Crossref). Threat: adjacent. Differentiation: Measures presence of policy in state under pressure, not what the worker does with it; we hold placement fixed and vary the amount of hierarchy shown, measuring behavioral departure and flagging. Complementary axes. Gist: Studies policy-carriage integrity: whether trusted policies remain present, sound and correctly bound in the agent's decision state immediately before action, under controlled context pressure over AutoGen/tau3 and OpenHands/SWE-bench traces. Protected placement preserves policy while task-local placement suffers eviction or weakening; a behavioral calibration found 0/90 unsafe proposals, so policy absence alone did not establish unsafe behavior; proposes ControlCapsule.
- **Devin can now Manage Devins**. The Cognition Team (2026). Cognition blog. https://cognition.com/blog/devin-can-now-manage-devins. Tier: should. Note: Web sweep 2026-09-03 (WebSearch, WebFetch, arXiv API, Crossref). Threat: none. Gist: Announces a coordinator mode in which one Devin session scopes work, assigns pieces to managed Devins, monitors progress, resolves conflicts and compiles results. Each managed Devin starts with a clean slate, a narrow focus and its own shell and test runner; the coordinator can read managed Devins' full trajectories and send instructions, context or corrections mid-task, and each child session is separately inspectable and messageable.
